XII 



 



Summary 
 
Hea exchangers are essen ial for empera ure regula ion across various 



indus ries, from everyday applica ions o space explora ion. Over ime, hese devices 



have evolved from simple clay vessels o complex s ruc ures made from advanced 



me al alloys and ceramics. In modern engineering, addi ive manufac uring (AM) has 



revolu ionized hea exchanger produc ion, enabling he crea ion of hin-walled 



s ruc ures wi h complex in ernal channels designed for efficien fluid flow. This is 



par icularly cri ical in indus ries such as aerospace, power genera ion, and 



manufac uring, where componen s mus be compac , ligh weigh , and capable of 



wi hs anding ex reme empera ures and pressures. However, radi ional AM me hods 



like laser powder bed fusion (L-PBF) are cons rained by size limi a ions, 



necessi a ing new manufac uring echniques o mee indus ry demands for compac 



large scale hea exchangers. 



This research addresses he challenges of developing he Laser Powder Direc ed 



Energy Deposi ion (LP-DED) process for ex reme-environmen hea exchangers. 



Process and flow es experimen s were conduc ed, along wi h comprehensive 



charac eriza ion of LP-DED-fabrica ed microchannels, which are hin-walled (1 mm) 



and capable of con aining cryogenic or high- empera ure pressurized fluids. The 



resul s from he research es ablishes LP-DED as a viable echnology for hea 



exchanger fabrica ion by addressing challenges rela ed o geome ry, wall hickness, 



surface ex ure, and he fluid dynamics of hese unique AM surfaces. Three key 



research ques ions guide he s udy: 



 



1. How is he surface ex ure of hea exchanger microchannels affec ed by he 



LP-DED fabrica ion process? 



2. Wha are he geome rical rela ionships and sensi ivi ies affec ing fluid flow 



performance if hea exchanger channels are manufac ured wi h he LP-



DED process? 



3. Wha improvemen s can be made o con rol he surface ex ure of hin-wall 



LP-DED in ernal microchannels? 



 



A de ailed li era ure review iden ifies significan gaps in curren hin-wall LP-



DED manufac uring and in ernal surface enhancemen echniques. An experimen al 



s udy examines LP-DED process mechanics and build parame ers, focusing on heir 



influence of he hin-wall surface ex ure and he effec of build angles on bo h open 



and closed s ruc ures. This research es ablishes guidelines for Design for Addi ive 



Manufac uring (DfAM), addressing process limi a ions, surface ex ure, and wall 



 hickness me rics for he LP-DED process. 



This research in roduces microchannels fabrica ed using LP-DED in various 



sizes, wi h heir in ernal and ex ernal surface ex ures, wall hicknesses, and 



repea abili y charac erized. These microchannels are hen processed in ernally using 



various surface enhancemen echniques o provide varia ions of he surface finish. 



Two experimen al s udies were conduc ed, wi h comprehensive charac eriza ion 



performed of he in ernal channel surfaces o evalua e he varia ions in surface ex ure 











XIII 



 



resul ing from he enhancemen processes and heir rela ionship o flow resis ance 



and fric ion fac ors. 



Key innova ions include he charac eriza ion of a new hydrogen-resis an alloy 



(NASA HR-1), which provides founda ional da a for hea exchanger design. The 



s udy also iden ifies surface ex ure mechanisms ha affec fluid fric ion fac ors, 



resul ing from dis inc enhancemen echniques such as peak smoo hing, roughness 



minimiza ion, waviness, and valley reduc ion. Addi ionally, fric ion fac ors and 



differen ial pressure in LP-DED-fabrica ed microchannels, bo h in as-buil and 



surface-enhanced condi ions, were inves iga ed. Surface rea men s such as abrasive

charac eriza ion led o he developmen of new correla ions for calcula ing he 



hydraulic diame er of square channels and predic ing sand grain roughness and 



fric ion fac ors. These correla ions resul ed in pressure drop predic ions wi h 



devia ions of less han 20% from experimen al da a, offering a 50% improvemen 



over previous models. 



 











XIV 



 



Samenva ing (Du ch) 
 



Warm ewisselaars zijn essen ieel voor empera uurregeling in verschillende 



indus rieën, van alledaagse oepassingen o ruim everkenning. In de loop van de ijd 



zijn deze appara en geëvolueerd van eenvoudige kleiva en o complexe s ruc uren 



gemaak van geavanceerde me aallegeringen en keramiek. In de moderne echniek 



heef addi ieve fabricage (AM) de produc ie van warm ewisselaars ge ransformeerd, 



waardoor de crea ie van dunwandige s ruc uren me complexe in erne kanalen 



mogelijk is gemaak , die zijn on worpen voor efficiën e vloeis ofs roming. Di is 



vooral cruciaal in sec oren zoals de luch - en ruim evaar , energieopwekking en 



produc ie, waar onderdelen compac , lich en bes and egen ex reme empera uren en 



drukken moe en zijn. Tradi ionele AM-me hoden, zoals laserpoederbedfusie (L-



PBF), hebben ech er beperkingen in groo e, waardoor nieuwe produc ie echnieken 



nodig zijn om e voldoen aan de indus riële vraag naar compac e groo schalige 



warm ewisselaars. 



Di onderzoek rich zich op de on wikkeling van he Laser Powder Direc ed 



Energy Deposi ion (LP-DED) proces voor warm ewisselaars die geschik zijn voor 



ex reme omgevingen. Proces- en s romings es s zijn ui gevoerd, samen me 



ui gebreide karak erisering van LP-DED-gefabriceerde microkanalen, die dunwandig 



zijn (1 mm) en in s aa zijn cryogene of hoog- empera uur-gepers e vloeis offen e 



beva en. De onderzoeksresul a en onen aan da LP-DED een levensva bare 



 echnologie is voor de fabricage van warm ewisselaars, door ui dagingen aan e 



pakken me be rekking o geome rie, wanddik e, oppervlak e ex uur en de 



vloeis ofdynamica van deze unieke AM-oppervlakken. Drie belangrijke 



onderzoeksvragen s uren de s udie: 



 



1. Hoe word de oppervlak eafwerking van warm ewisselermicrokanalen 



beïnvloed door he LP-DED-fabricageproces? 



2. Wa zijn de geome rische rela ies en gevoeligheden die de pres a ie van de 



vloeis ofs room beïnvloeden als warm ewisselarkanalen worden 



vervaardigd me he LP-DED-proces?

1.4 THE LP-DED PROCESS 



LP-DED (Figure 1.7) is a process in which a laser beam is direc ed o a powder 



s ream which is blown in i s focus forming a mel pool which subsequen ly solidifies 



 hus forming he par by deposi ion of successive layers [73]. The blown powder 



deposi ion head and he laser beam op ics are moun ed on a gan ry (or robo ) o allow 



for he precise mo ion con rol needed o manufac ure hin-wall fea ures [74]. The 



laser is ypically cen ral o he in egra ed deposi ion head and he powder being blown 



is concen ric wi h he laser. The blown powder deposi ion nozzles can be loca ed in 



a coaxial annular ring or hey are separa e nozzles, ypically 3 or 4, spaced equally 



around he laser beam [75]. 



The LP-DED process allows large scale fabrica ion (>3 me ers) using me al 



powder feeds ock, only limi ed by he robo ic or Car esian gan ry sys em [76]. 



Freeform s ruc ures are fabrica ed based on oolpa hs genera ed from CAD models. 



The size of he powder par icles and par icle size dis ribu ions (PSD) can vary from 



coarse (45 – 105 µm) o fine (15 – 45 µm) [75]. Examples of LP-DED applica ions 



include cas ing and forging replacemen s, cladding and repair, fabrica ion of primary 



s ruc ures, as well as hin-wall large diame er s ruc ures such as hea exchangers and 



rocke nozzles wi h in egral channels [5,77]. However, he manufac uring of hin-



wall componen s wi h in egral channels wi h LP-DED is a a low ma uri y level: he 



charac eriza ion of he process is limi ed and DfAM prac ices are no es ablished. 



As indica ed in Figure 1.6, Laser Powder DED (LP-DED) can overcome he 



limi a ion regarding overall objec size affec ing L-PBF, while s ill allowing o 



incorpora e hin-wall (1 mm) in ernal flow passages or microchannels in o he par 



[13,78]. 



The work repor ed in his disser a ion provides design engineers wi h 



informa ion abou process limi a ions, design guidelines, pos -processing 



requiremen s, and experimen al es da a rela ed o he manufac uring of 



microchannels wi h LP-DED.

1.7 AIM OF THE RESEARCH 



Using he LP-DED process o manufac ure microchannel hea exchangers, 



especially for ex reme environmen applica ions, in roduces challenges rela ed o 



consis en geome ry, wall hickness, and high surface ex ure. While radi ional 



manufac uring me hods like machining do no ypically have issues wi h surface 



roughness and ex ure, adop ing AM for hea exchangers presen s new challenges.

3.1 ABSTRACT 



This experimen al s udy comple ed a design of experimen s (DOE) o de ermine

samples. This s udy summarizes he charac eriza ion work of he LP-DED process 



for 1 mm enclosed walls wi h an Fe-Ni-Cr (NASA HR-1) alloy. The LP-DED 



parame ers including laser power, powder feedra e, ravel speed, layer heigh , and 



ro ary a omized powder feeds ock were modified in he experimen . An evalua ion of 



 he DOE samples and resul ing surface ex ure is provided along wi h conclusions 



from hese experimen s. Resul s indica e ha 3D areal and 2D profile (direc ional) 



surface ex ure is es ima ed by 2x he powder diame er ha becomes cap ured or 



par ially mel ed on he railing edge of he mel pool. The fine powder showed a 



higher sensi ivi y o parame er changes bu resul ed in a higher densi y ma erial and 



23% reduc ion in roughness. Surface ex ure was also shown o vary be ween closed 



channel shapes (in ernal) due o ricoche s, recircula ion, and higher volume of 



powder available o bond compared o ex ernal (ou er) surfaces. The unders anding 



of he LP-DED process as-buil surface ex ure is cri ical o fluid flow applica ions 



such as hea exchanges and can modify performance for enhanced hea ransfer or 



can be a de rimen o pressure drop.

3.3 METHODOLOGY 



 



The LP-DED process uses a laser beam o crea e a mel pool and me al powder 



is injec ed in o his pool using an iner carrier gas hrough a deposi ion head. As he 



deposi ion head raverses an area ha was deposi ed he ma erial cools and hen 



solidifies crea ing he bead. The deposi ion head is a ached o a mo ion con rol 



sys em, such as a mul i-axis robo ic arm or Car esian coordina e gan ry sys em. The 



Car esian coordina e gan ry sys em is preferred and was used in his s udy allowing 











Chap er 3 



 



 



Chap er 3—39 



 



for be er accuracy and repea abili y, which is cri ical for single-wid h bead wall

deposi ed un il he final par is crea ed. LP-DED also has he advan age of using 



mul iple axis for complex geome ries and is no limi ed o only 3-axes. 



The samples crea ed for his s udy were single-wid h bead race racks wi h 75 



mm leng h, 25 mm in heigh , and 1 mm wall hickness (adjacen walls spaced 25 mm 



apar ). These were deposi ed as a race rack as opposed o a frees anding wall o 



provide geome ric s abili y and elimina e any dis or ion. Each sample was deposi ed 



according o he parame ers es ablished for he DOE and hen measured using non-



con ac microscopic imaging o evalua e roughness and ex ure. Fur her 



charac eriza ion of he wall hickness and porosi y was evalua ed using microscopic 



and scanning elec ron microscope (SEM) imaging. 



The LP-DED sys em has several subsys ems ha include he powder injec ion 



and purge gas flow sys em, he laser power supply and op ics, and he mo ion con rol. 



Each of hese subsys ems mus work congruen ly o provide he desired bead, 



resul ing geome ry, and ma erial charac eris ics. These subsys ems use suppor 



hardware and inpu parame ers ha can be adjus ed o affec he ma erial 



charac eris ics, including surface roughness. The laser and op ical sys em include 



many of he laser parame ers and op ical hardware ha derive he beam delivery 



including he power, spo size, focus posi ion, profile of beam, ype of laser, and laser 



opera ional mode (such as con inuous or pulsed). The powder injec ion sys em 



includes sys ems and inpu s such as he powder feedra e, ype of carrier gas, core 



shielding gas and/or secondary gas flow (mass flow ra e and gas ype), and he 



powder nozzle design (annular or mul i-nozzle). The mo ion con rol sys em 



manipula es he movemen parame er for he deposi ion head ha includes he ravel 



speed, se s layer heigh , s andoff dis ance, angle of he head, and ha ching (or s ep-



over). While hese parame ers are fundamen al o he basic build geome ry, o her 



fac ors may be more specific o componen build geome ry, such as spacing be ween 



fea ures, build pla e configura ion, and oolpa h build s ra egy. The major subsys ems 



and parame ers for LP-DED are shown in Figure 3.1.

3.3.2 NASA HR-1 Ma erial

alloy for use in hydrogen based hea exchanger applica ions [201]. The NASA HR-1 



alloy was developed a NASA Marshall Space Fligh Cen er in he la e 1990’s as a 



hydrogen environmen resis an (ie. “HR”) superalloy for use in high empera ure and 



high pressure hydrogen applica ions [205]. NASA HR-1 is a Fe-Ni-Cr Superalloy 



 ha provides high s reng h and high duc ili y in hese harsh environmen s and 



originally derived from JBK-75. The alloy has been demons ra ed in several 



applica ions focused on liquid rocke nozzles and high empera ure hydrogen 



environmen hea exchangers [77]. While o her alloys exis ha could mee hese 



general requiremen s, such as A-286, JBK-75, and he 300 series s ainless s eel (304, 



310, 316), hey can be limi ed in s reng h for such hea exchanger applica ions [13]. 



Two powder lo s (HRA9 and HRA4) of ro ary a omized NASA HR-1 from 



Homogenized Me als Inc. (HMI) were used for he sample deposi ion in a fine cu

3.3.4 Roughness Measuremen s and Microscopy 



 Each sample was hen measured using a non-con ac Keyence VR-5200

from his region. Waviness was ob ained in he horizon al and ver ical direc ions wi h

on he sidewall ver ical surface roughness, which is in he direc ion of flow for hea 



exchanger applica ions. The measuremen uncer ain y was ob ained by repea ing 



measuremen s on a single sample for each fine and coarse powder PSD samples. A 



99% confidence in erval for measuremen uncer ain y is calcula ed and presen ed in

In addi ion o areal surface analysis, five ver ical lines and hree horizon al line 



profiles were ob ained from he surface areal profile. The ver ical direc ion

profile ex ure measuremen s (Pa, Pz, Pp, Pv, Psk, Pku) were used for his experimen 



which is inclusive of waviness and roughness ha could impac he applica ion for 



flow in hea exchangers. This is synonymous wi h he radi ional Ra (average 



roughness) bu are no fil ered for waviness. The line profile da a is impor an o he 



end applica ion, and direc ionali y should be considered for specific applica ions 



[208], such as fluid flow, and included as an ou come of his s udy. The 2D direc ional

 The process hardware and se up for building he desired wall hickness can 



have an impac on he final surface roughness. The mel pool resul ing in 1 mm wall 



 hickness resul s in a lower ca chmen efficiency of 5-20% [120]. The powder ha is 



cap ured in he mel pool is of en drawn o he cen er due o Marangoni flow [75].

The finer powder has more ins ances of subsurface par icles han he coarse

from he nozzle and causes rebounds off solidified surfaces [223]. The coarse

3.6 CONCLUSIONS 



 



Indus rializa ion of addi ively manufac ured par s requires a de ailed 



charac eriza ion of he micros ruc ure and he resul ing geome ry such as wall 



 hickness, defec s such as porosi y, and he surface ex ure for successful use in 



cri ical applica ions. In his s udy, closed race rack NASA HR-1 1 mm hin-wall 



samples were buil using he LP-DED process wi h ro ary a omized fine and coarse 



powder o evalua e surface ex ure (roughness and waviness), basic geome ry, and 



micros ruc ure. These samples were successfully buil using a single-bead deposi ion 



wall o a heigh of 25 mm. A design of experimen s (DOE) was se up o evalua e he 



varia ion in parame ers o de ermine he correla ion and influence of he build 



parame ers and powder par icle size dis ribu ion (PSD). A o al of 35 samples were 











Chap er 3 



 



 



Chap er 3—63 



 



successfully deposi ed wi h various parame ers and were measured using a non-



con ac pa erned ligh projec ion profilome er in he as-buil condi ion. The samples 



were also evalua ed using op ical microscopy and scanning elec ron microscope 



(SEM) and sec ioned o evalua e hickness, porosi y, and roughness. The resul s are 



presen ed and summarized, and he following conclusions are drawn from he NASA 



HR-1, 1 mm hin-wall samples. 



• Surface roughness of LP-DED ma erial is derived from excess or par ially 



mel ed powder cap ured on he ou mos surface of he railing edge mel 



pool. The areal average surface roughness (Sa) is hus domina ed by peaks 



of hese par icles. 



• Par ially or unmel ed powder can be solidified in he subsurface and was 



observed o be a maximum of 2x he diame er of he powder PSD. 



• Fine powder (10-45 µm) produces on average roughness 23% lower 



compared o he coarse powder (45-105 µm). 



• The inner (enclosed) surface shows a higher average surface roughness, 



 ex ure, and waviness compared o he ou er surface due o ricoche s, 



recircula ion, and higher volume of powder available o bond o he surface 



during solidifica ion. This is fur her exaspera ed wi h hin-wall deposi ions 



where powder efficiency is reduced and more powder available o bond o 



 he ou mos surface during solidifica ion. 



• The resul ing surface roughness from he fine powder indica ed a higher 



sensi ivi y o he parame er changes where he roughness wi h he coarse 



powder showed li le change. 



• The analysis of variance indica ed ha he powder, layer heigh , and 



in erac ion of hese accoun ed for 90% of he inner surface roughness and 



95% of he ou er surface roughness. 



• The fine powder produced samples wi h lower overall porosi y and averaged 



0.03% ±0.01%, while he coarse samples had a higher range and averaged 



0.13% ±0.07% 



• A wall hickness of 1.05 o 1.15 mm can be properly main ained wi h ei her 



coarse or fine powder and rela ed o he laser power. 



• Waviness was minimized using a lower laser powder (<333 W), higher layer 



heigh (<0.28 mm) along wi h increased ravel speed (800 mm/min) and 



increased powder feed ra e (24.2 grams/min) o produce a uniform surface 



wi h minor waviness. The ypically repor ed menisci of layering were no 



observed in he NASA HR-1 hin-wall samples deposi ed wi h he op imized 



parame ers and 3-nozzle configura ion. 



• Surface roughness can be minimized by using fine powder, laser power of 



350 W, powder feedra e of 23 g/min, ravel speed of 762 mm/min, and a low 



layer heigh of 0.229 mm. While he surface roughness is reduced a hese 



parame ers a balance should also be evalua ed based on process economics. 



Wi h he decreased layer heigh , he build ime is increased by 17%. The 



layer heigh could be increased o 0.254 mm, and he impac on surface 



roughness would be a 6% increase (+1 µm).

4.1 ABSTRACT 



The charac eriza ion of geome ric capabili ies and limi a ions is cri ical for 



es ablishing guidelines for end users of he echnology. Wi hin his s udy, several

wi h 1 mm- hick walls and varying inclina ion angles up o 45° using he NASA HR-

irregulari ies causing ma erial droop, and 3) excess ma erial. The experimen revealed

upskin roughness reaches a maximum peak a 20° and slowly reduces as powder 



adheres wi hin valleys. Bo h he downskin and upskin surface ex ures are domina ed 



by irregular waviness genera ed by he mel pool.

4.5 CONCLUSIONS 



I was shown ha he LP-DED process can produce low porosi y (<0.14%) 



angled walls wi h arge hickness of 1 mm and wi h an inclina ion angle of up o 45° 



using he NASA HR-1 (Fe-Ni-Cr) alloy. The geome ric aspec s of he samples 



including wall hickness, mel pool, and surface ex ure were evalua ed. The surface 



 ex ure was fil ered o properly segmen he waviness and roughness parame ers o 



de ermine heir con ribu ion and he causing process mechanisms. From he 



experimen al observa ions, op ical micrographs, SEM images, and op ical surface 



 ex ure measuremen s, he following conclusions are drawn: 



 



• Walls fea uring angles of up o 30° are s able, bu angles above ha 



 hreshold pose higher risks of a build failure. In such cases, i may be 



necessary o incorpora e adjacen wall s iffening o ensure s ruc ural 



in egri y. 



• The primary areal surface ex ure (Sa) exhibi s an increase wi h he rise in 



wall angle, which can be a ribu ed o surface irregulari ies arising from he 



mel pool and ma erial droop. Peaks are dominan for he downskin surface 



wi h he increasing angle and reduced for he upskin. 











Chap er 4 



 



Chap er 4—87 



 



• The waviness and subsequen wall hickness increases wi h he increased 



wall angle due o he chao ic surface irregulari ies from ma erial droop on 



 he downskin. The upskin waviness is driven by he excess ma erial 



deposi ion above he nominal mel pool. This is due o direc impingemen 



of he par icles and remel ed ma erial flowing upwards and ou wards. 



• Proper fil ering of he ex ure measuremen s is required o segrega e 



roughness and waviness. Differing conclusions could be drawn abou he 



mechanisms leading o ex ure if proper fil ering is no considered. The λc 



of 0.8 mm was an appropria e cu -off fil er o ex rac direc ional roughness 



and waviness for hese hin-wall samples. 



• As he angle increases, he fil ered average areal and direc ional surface 



roughness (Sa, Ra) of he downskin surface significan ly decreases. This 



reduc ion can be a ribu ed o a decrease in he number of adhered par icles 



on he surface. This rend con radic s he observa ions documen ed in he 



li era ure regarding surfaces manufac ured wi h he L-PBF process. 



• The upskin surface exhibi s an increase in average areal (Sa) wi h an 



increase in par icle coun . The roughness cres s a 20° and remains cons an 



un il he adhered powder filled in voids, resul ing in a reduc ion in roughness 



a higher build angles. 



• The wall hickness increases exponen ially wi h he angle, reaching an 



inflec ion poin a 30° where he hickness s ar s o significan ly increase due 



 o mel pool droop. A correla ion for he es ima ion of he wall hickness 



was developed using he da a rela ed o he samples made of NASA HR-1 



alloy. 



• The resul s ob ained from single-pass angled walls indica e ha , for LP-



DED, he direc ional maximum profile heigh (Rz) can be es ima ed using 



 he maximum par icle size dis ribu ion. Only par icles wi h size less han 85 



µm adhere o he downskin surface a angles above 35° due o higher 



rebounds and recircula ion. 



• The s air s epping ypically observed in he LP-DED was no de ec ed wi h 



a layer heigh of 0.254 mm and remel ing of 4-5 previously deposi ed layers. 



 



The complex geome ry of a par being fabrica ed inheren ly al ers surface ex ure 



due o varied angles. This change is influenced by he build parame ers and resul ing 



mel pool. Thickness varia ion and powder adherence for upskin and downskin follow 



comparable rends based on wall angle, driven by process mechanisms. Surface 



 ex ure, encompassing roughness and waviness, varies wi h alloy, parame ers, and 



resul ing LP-DED mel pool. Designers mus adhere o geome ry guidelines and 



charac erize surface ex ure, which can impac fa igue life, corrosion, hea ransfer, 



and fluid flow performance. Previous views on LP-DED manda ed pos -machining, 



bu experimen s show properly designed LP-DED can approach L-PBF surface 



finishes. LP-DED manufac uring holds po en ial for aerospace, power genera ion, 



and indus rial hea exchangers.

5.5 SURFACE TEXTURE 



Due o he impor ance of surface ex ure from he componen and sys em

s udy in Chap er 3 evalua ed he surface ex ure of he LP-DED process using 1 mm

Using fine powder reduced he roughness by 23% compared o coarse powder. There 



was also a discernible difference in surface ex ure be ween he inner surface 



( rapping addi ional powder) and he ou er (ex ernal) surface. This dis inc ion was 



due o he enclosed volume rapping excess powder ha recircula ed in o he mel 



pool as i solidified. I 's impor an o no e ha while he samples in Chap ers 4 and 5 



were hin-walled, he spacing be ween walls was abou 25 mm, where hea exchanger 



designs wi h microchannels would be less han 10 mm. 



The primary average areal surface ex ure, Sa, was measured for each sample on 



bo h he inner (in ernal) and ou er (ex ernal) surfaces. Sa was chosen o provide a

heigh and densi y and a ra io is proposed. This research resul ed in horough surface

6.3.2 Surface Enhancemen Processes 



Several in ernal surface enhancemen processes were selec ed wi h he ul ima e 



goal of es ing he possibili ies of improving he flow fric ion charac eris ics of hea 



exchangers channels. These included abrasive flow machining (AFM), chemical 



mechanical polishing (CMP), chemical milling (CM), pulsed elec rochemical 



machining (PECM), and hermal energy me hod (TEM) or hermal deburring. These 



enhancemen processes were selec ed among he many available 



[126,133,142,149,271], based on he abili y o modify in ernal microchannels wi h a 



cross sec ion of less han 2.54 x 2.54 mm [51,131,146,272]. The primary goal of his 



research was no o minimize surface roughness or o allow for he bes possible 



surface polishing of he channels, bu o analyze he varying levels of surface 



polishing ha can be ob ained o gain a be er unders anding of how he polishing can 



be uned o he requiremen s in erms of flow resis ance. 



AFM uses a viscous fluid polymer ha con ains suspended abrasive par icles. 



The slurry is pressurized and pumped hrough he channels using a single or bi-



direc ional flow [273,274], hus providing abrasion, and he removed par icula e is 



carried away wi h he media. This process is also referred o as ex rude-honing or 



slurry-honing. The slurry ype, volume, ime, and number of cycles were adjus ed o 



modify he channel surfaces. The pressure was 68.9 bar and he flow was 



unidirec ional and he flow ra e ranged from 0.56 L/min o 4.47 L/min. Following he 



AFM process, he slurry was removed wi h compressed air and rinsed wi h a cleaning 



solu ion. The channels were hen dried wi h compressed air, ul rasonically cleaned, 



rinsed wi h wa er, and again dried. 



CM, or chemical polishing, consis s of flowing a chemical solu ion hrough he 



channels ha a acks he grain s ruc ure and dissolves he me al a he surface 



[127,135]. The dissolved ma erial is flushed ou wi h he hydrofluoric solu ion. 



Longer exposure imes yield more ma erial removal. The empera ure of he solu ion 



pumped hrough he channels varied from 52 o 60°C and he surfaces were exposed 



 o he solu ion for a period ha ranges from 4 o 19 mins. The par s were rinsed 



following he chemical milling. 



CMP is a process in which chemical and mechanical forces are applied o remove 



surface ma erial along preferen ial direc ions hus reducing he peaks heigh [275]. 



The chemical solu ion removes mos of he ma erial, while abrasive media planarizes 



 he surface. The chemical solu ion and micro-abrasives were flowed hrough he 



channels and any removed par icles flushed wi h he solu ion. The flow ra e of he 



chemical solu ion, empera ure, ime of flow, and weigh of he microabrasives was 



varied for each channel. The flow ra e was varied from 0.4 o 2 L/min a empera ures 



of 50 o 60°C and runs wi h and wi hou microabrasives (up o 10%). The exposure 



 ime was also varied be ween 10 o 20 mins. 



PECM ob ains he surface improvemen by applying a local direc curren (DC) 



pulse using an elec rode o dissolve me al [276]. The elec rode was inser ed inside of 



 he channel and dragged hrough, hus providing he proper offse gap. A limi ed 



por ion of he elec rode was exposed. The channel was ac ively flushed wi h an 



elec roly ic sodium chloride solu ion. 











Chap er 6 



 



Chap er 6—109 



 



TEM is a process where par s are placed in a sealed chamber, which is 



pressurized wi h a mix ure of gaseous fuel and oxygen, and igni ed [277,278]. The 



high empera ure of he combus ion process vaporizes ( hin) areas wi h low hermal 



mass. The par was fix ured o preven damage in he 25 cm diame er chamber 



opera ing a a combus ion pressure of 6 bar wi h a o al processing ime of 30 seconds. 



The surface enhancemen processes were ou sourced o commercial vendors 



ac ively working produc ion. Prior o conduc ing he flow es ing and 



charac eriza ion, each vendor received developmen samples for rials. This s ep was 



necessary because bo h ma erial composi ion and geome ry can influence he 



parame ers used. Al hough general parame ers were ou lined above in Sec ion 2.2,

in he surfaces of he channels ra her han achieving an ideally "smoo h" surface. To

nearly he en ire surface of he sample, while he coarse powder par icles cover abou

The SEM images for he AFM samples indica e ha a high number of par icles 



can be iden ified also af er processing. Several of he samples such as AFM-A1,

ma erial or smeared. On a limi ed number of AFM samples such as AFM-A4, AFM-

7.5 CONCLUSIONS 



This chap er presen ed he resul s of flow es ing for 600 mm leng h channels, 



represen a ive of LP-DED hea exchanger square channels. The effec s of hree 



surface enhancemen me hods—abrasive flow machining, chemical milling, and 



chemical mechanical polishing— on he fluid dynamic performance of hea 



exchanger channels were compared. As-buil channels were used o de ermine he 



performance benchmark. All he buil and es ed channels fea ure square a cross 



sec ion measuring 2.54 x 2.54 mm. The channels were fabrica ed from he NASA

nozzles, however resul s are applicable o hea exchangers realized wi h he LP-DED 



process in general. 



Flow es ing was conduc ed a various mass flow ra es o charac erize he 



pressure drop for each channel. Following experimen al es ing, he sample channels 



underwen des ruc ive evalua ion, whereby inner surfaces were charac erized using 



SEM, op ical microscopy, and op ical surface ex ure analysis. Based on his de ailed 



s udy, a fric ion fac or correla ion was developed. The correla ion includes 



 ex ure-dependen parame ers which in urn rela e o equivalen sand grain roughness. 



The sand grain roughness, originally based on he experimen s of Darcy and 



Nikuradse, is commonly used for fric ion fac or correla ions. However, surfaces 



produced by he LP-DED process are more complex and canno be charac erized wi h 



sand grains values. 



The following conclusions can be drawn: 



• Surface enhancemen s significan ly reduced boundary layer urbulence by 



elimina ing adhered powder and planarizing he surface, resul ing in a 



 enfold reduc ion in pressure drop. 



• The surface enhancemen echniques [abrasive flow machining (AFM), 



chemical milling (CM), and chemical mechanical polishing (CMP)] produce 



dis inc surface a ribu es (peaks and valleys associa ed wi h roughness and 



waviness) due o differences in he way ma erial is removed, hus surface 



flow drag is differen . 



• The surface ex ure was varied across he channels using enhancemen s 



applied o he as-buil surfaces. The fil ered Ra ranged from 3.6 o 19.3 µm, 



primary Sa ranged from 11.3 o 31.6 µm, primary Sk ranging from 33.9 o 



99.8 µm, and Pp from 31.4 o 152.3 µm. 



• A new defini ion of hydraulic diame er, dependen on he value of he 



charac erized cross-sec ional area and core surface ex ure (Sk), has been 



proposed and valida ed wi h experimen al da a. This defini ion reduces he 



error associa ed wi h radi ional defini ions of hydraulic diame er, which 



depend solely on cross-sec ional area and perime er. This new expression of 



 he hydraulic diame er allows o overcome he large error due o he s rong 



biases occurring in he case of highly ex ured surfaces. For example, he 



value of he ac ual perime er of he cross-sec ion of as-buil channels is 



widely differen from i s design value, which assumes a perfec geome ry, 



free from any irregulari y. 



• None of he fric ion fac or correla ions documen ed in he li era ure provide 



values ha ma ch hose compu ed wi h he Darcy fric ion fac or ob ained 



from measured differen ial pressures, including he Colebrook-Whi e 



correla ion. A new fric ion fac or correla ion was ob ained by modifying

of he new correla ion has been valida ed. 



• The varia ion in he surface profile correla es o four dis inc ypes of ex ure 



and rela es o he reduc ion in he experimen al fric ion fac or: 1) roughness

Chap er 7—186 



 



peaks, 2) peak smoo hing/reduc ion, 3) minimized roughness and combined

• A me hod was developed o predic sand grain roughness based on varia ions

• The proposed me hod for charac erizing equivalen sand grain roughness

8 Summary and Recommenda ions 



8.1 MAIN FINDINGS AND CONCLUSIONS 



 



The research documen ed in his hesis concerns he laser powder direc ed energy 



deposi ion (LP-DED) process for fabrica ing large-scale compac hea exchangers. 



Wi h an upper limi for he build volume ha is over 17 imes larger han he larges 



possible wi h powder bed fusion machines (as of 2024), LP-DED enables he 



fabrica ion of large and highly complex par s, enhancing performance, par 



consolida ion, and he abili y o process novel alloys. This inves iga ion encompassed 



comprehensive charac eriza ion and experimen al es ing of LP-DED-fabrica ed 



microchannels, as well as pos -process surface enhancemen s, focusing on cri ical 



surface ex ure and i s impac on fluid flow. The findings are essen ial o bring o 



widespread adop ion of a manufac uring solu ion ha addresses challenges rela ed o 



consis en geome ry, wall hickness, and high surface ex ure, es ablishing LP-DED 



as a viable echnology for curren and fu ure hea exchanger fabrica ion. To 



summarize he main resul s, each key research ques ion is addressed separa ely in he 



following. 



 



RQ 1: How is he surface ex ure of hea exchanger 



microchannels affec ed by he LP-DED fabrica ion process? 
 



The surface ex ure of LP-DED walls is influenced by he build process 



parame ers and feeds ock including inpu power, ravel speed, layer heigh , powder 



feed ra e, and powder size dis ribu ion. Chap er 3 and Chap er 4 address his research 



ques ion by rea ing experimen s and he des ruc ive evalua ions of samples which 



enabled he full charac eriza ion of he rela ionship be ween process parame ers, 



geome ric cons rain s, and he mechanisms causing he surface ex ure. The focus was 



on he reduc ion of he as-buil surface ex ure, which is five o seven imes rougher 



 han ha of radi ionally machined surfaces. Fine powder was shown o reduce 



surface ex ure bu resul ed in larger varia ions of surface ex ure and bulk densi y 



compared o coarse powder. Geome rical s udies revealed ha wall angles exceeding 



30° require s iffening or suppor s o preven failure, wi h surface ex ure largely 



influenced by powder adherence and mel pool irregulari ies. 



 



The charac eriza ion of he surface ex ure resul ing from LP-DED requires he 



iden ifica ion and measuremen of key parame ers. These include bo h hose deriving 



Chap er 8 
 











Chap er 8 



 



Chap er 8—188 



 



from 3D areal surface measuremen s and 2D direc ional (legacy) measuremen s, 



fil ered according o he necessary surface opography and ISO s andards (ISO 4288-

roughness, and waviness. The s udy evalua ed: average ex ure (Sa), core roughness

direc ional parame ers including average, maximum heigh , and peak roughness (Ra, 



Rz, Rp, Rpk), primary, average, and maximum profile (Pp, Pa, Pz), and average and

conclusion ha while many s andard heigh (ampli ude) parame ers were relevan for

necessary o fully evalua e surface opography and correla e i wi h flow 



performance. 



 



The build process is significan ly influenced by several key parame ers. The 



s udy repor ed in Chap er 3 focused on he produc ion of boxes wi h 1 mm wall 



 hickness. The as-buil inner and ou er surfaces were charac erized by varying 



machine parame ers and powder sizes. Cri ical machine parame ers included mo ion 



con rol, laser and op ics, and powder feed and deposi ion. All build parame ers were 



evalua ed using a design of experimen s (DOE) o unders and con ribu ions o surface 



 ex ure and in erac ions. Key build parame ers ha were iden ified were inpu power, 



 ravel speed, layer heigh , powder feed ra e, and powder size dis ribu ion. Fine 



powder (10-45 µm) and coarse powder (45-105 µm) feeds ock made hrough ro ary 



a omiza ion were used. Des ruc ive charac eriza ion of samples revealed ha 3D areal 



and 2D profile surface ex ure correla e wi h wice he measured powder diame er or 



wi h he diame er of par ially mel ed powder a he mel pool's railing edge. 



 



Several adjus men s can be made o he LP-DED echnique o enhance he build 



process and improve surface ex ure. Surface roughness s ems from excess or 



par ially mel ed powder adhered o he railing edge of he mel pool in addi ion o 



mel pool irregulari ies. Fine powder (10-45 µm) reduces average surface ex ure by 



23% compared o coarse powder (45-105 µm) bu resul s in a higher degree of 



variabili y of he ex ure. Inner surfaces exhibi higher roughness due o ricoche s and 



recircula ion, par icularly in hin-wall deposi ions where powder efficiency is 



reduced o 10-20% (i.e., only 10-20% of he blown powder is cap ured in he mel 



pool). Analysis of variance indica ed ha powder size, layer heigh , and heir 



in erac ion accoun s for 90% of inner surface roughness and 95% of ou er surface 



roughness. Fine powder also resul ed in lower overall porosi y (0.03% ±0.01%) 



compared o coarse powder (0.13% ±0.07%). 



 



To minimize waviness and achieve uniform surfaces, op imal parame ers include 



a lower laser power (<333 W), higher layer heigh (<0.28 mm), increased ravel speed 



(800 mm/min), and increased powder feed ra e (24.2 g/min). Specific op imal 



parame ers for minimizing surface roughness are fine powder, laser power of 350 W, 



powder feed ra e of 23 g/min, ravel speed of 762 mm/min, and a low layer heigh of 



0.229 mm. Al hough hese se ings reduce surface roughness, process economics

Chap er 8—189 



 



mus also be considered. When he layer heigh is decreased by 10%, he build ime 



increases by 17%, while he roughness only increases by 6% (+1 µm).
